<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01WddDNG4z4GfMphjcKuDPpk`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When synthetic text is recursively fed back as input, the resulting multi-generational content degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively prompting a fixed model to generate from its own prior outputs); we study prompt-chains, which isolate text-intrinsic information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	\begin{figure*}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}
13	\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}
14	\label{fig:pipeline}
15	\end{figure*}
16	
17	This gap cuts across multiple communities.
18	Binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).
19	Membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.
20	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.
21	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
22	
23	Knowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.
24	For data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principled choice.
25	Model collapse theory predicts monotonic quality degradation across generations, but existing analyses are population-level and provide no per-text measure of where degradation saturates.
26	$\Kempirical$ fills this gap by localizing the generation at which adjacent outputs become empirically indistinguishable.
27	
28	The Data Processing Inequality implies that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$; its actual value is determined empirically.
29	We present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.
30	We operationalize $\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \geq 1$ where the pairwise $\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\ref{sec:formulation}).
31	
32	Our contributions:
33	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
34	  \item \textbf{First empirical measurement of $\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$: 4/6 families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 ($p \leq 0.002$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.008$ $\auc$ for generator identity).
35	  Zero-shot frontier LLMs likewise collapse beyond $\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\% (vs.\ 20\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Section~\ref{sec:why_kstar}).
36	  \item \textbf{Domain ordering}: arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across tested configurations; code (Python) matches web text depth despite a different modality, confirmed by multi-source pooling (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
37	  \item \textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-independent features within continuation mode enable 80--92\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\rho > 0.90$; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\Kempirical$ assignments are fragile under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$); as a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\Kempirical$ correctly predicts binary ${>}$ graduated filtering for web text (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
38	\end{enumerate}
39	
```

379	\label{sec:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}

383	\small
384	\caption{Bootstrap 95\% CI for arXiv cross-scorer (Pythia-1.4B) 2v3 boundary $\auc$ (10{,}000 resamples). Point estimates classify 3/4 generators as $\Kempirical{=}3$; strict CI lower-bound criterion confirms 2/4.}
385	\label{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}
386	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
387	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
388	\toprule
389	\textbf{Generator} & \textbf{2v3 $\auc$} & \textbf{95\% CI} & \textbf{CI lower $> \theta$} \\
390	\midrule
391	Qwen-1.5B    & 0.632 & $[0.617,\,0.646]$ & Yes \\
392	LLaMA-3.1-8B & 0.620 & $[0.605,\,0.636]$ & Yes (0.5\,pp margin) \\
393	Mistral-7B   & 0.608 & $[0.593,\,0.622]$ & No \\
394	Qwen-7B      & 0.599 & $[0.584,\,0.613]$ & No \\
395	\bottomrule
396	\end{tabular}}
397	\end{table}
398	
399	\subsection{JSD Decay Analysis}
400	\label{sec:jsd_extended}
401	
402	The token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two distinct decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}).
403	For C4 web text, 0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$ across all 8 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$ (median cliff ratio ${\sim}$15$\times$), and subsequent pairs converge to a noise floor of ${\sim}$0.003--0.005.
404	Fitting $\jsd(k, k{+}1) \propto e^{-\lambda k}$ yields $\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$ for C4 (mean ${\approx}\,2.9$), consistent with rapid mixing of $G_\delta$ toward its stationary distribution.
405	These decay rates represent qualitative characterizations from 5-point curves rather than precise estimates.
406	The empirical decay rate $\lambda$ is consistent within each domain but varies across domains: C4 yields $\lambda \approx 2.9$, Wikipedia $\lambda \approx 3.1$ (excluding OLMo, whose $\lambda = 6.3$ reflects a capacity-limited collapse: 0v1 $\jsd = 0.169$ with near-complete information loss at the first step, the exponential fit is therefore unreliable), and arXiv $\lambda \approx 1.0$.
407	This cross-model consistency indicates that the contraction rate of $G_\delta$ is governed primarily by the structure of the input domain rather than the model architecture.
408	ArXiv text decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower than C4 ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$), with a correspondingly higher convergence floor (${\sim}$0.007--0.009 vs.\ ${\sim}$0.003--0.004).
409	Formulaic structural features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, section templates) degrade more slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth-distinguishing signal, this accounts for the elevated $\Kempirical{=}3$.
410	The retrain-chain exhibits a fundamentally different profile: $\lambda = 0.285$ with a cliff ratio of only 1.31$\times$ and no sharp drop between adjacent pairs (Figure~\ref{fig:retrain_decay}).
411	This gradual, sustained divergence reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts rather than text-intrinsic information loss, further supporting the distinction between prompt-chain and retrain-chain $\Kempirical$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:boundary_extended}).
412	
413	\begin{figure}[htbp]
414	\centering

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/experiments_c1.tex:43:Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay} show $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for 7/9 continuation-mode C4 configurations across four of six tested architecture families (4 Qwen + 3 non-Qwen; 1v2 $\auc$ range: 0.6112--0.6539; all CI lower bounds $> \theta$; permutation $p < 0.001$).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/experiments_c1.tex:74:However, boundary-level transfer is fragile: at 7B, 4/6 off-diagonal 1v2 pairs fall below $\theta$, so $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments do not transfer at the boundary even as overall discriminability improves with scale.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/experiments_c1.tex:176:$\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for web text holds across four of six families (1B--32B), four decoding strategies, and multi-source pools (Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B: $\Kempirical{=}1$; binary detectors: $K^*_{\text{binary}} \leq 1$, Table~\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:19:Several configurations are near-boundary (CI margins 0.2--4.5\,pp above $\theta$), and cross-model transfer at 7B preserves aggregate accuracy (96--100\% pairwise AUC retention) but not boundary-level assignments (4/6 off-diagonal pairs fall below $\theta$), underscoring that $\Kempirical{=}2$ is a robust population-level finding but fragile at the per-configuration level.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/introduction.tex:34:  \item \textbf{First empirical measurement of $\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$: 4/6 families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 ($p \leq 0.002$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.008$ $\auc$ for generator identity).

| C4 | Pythia-1.4B | 2 | 2 | .654 (1v2) | .654 (1v2) | +0.1pp |
| arXiv | Pythia-1.4B | 3 | 3 | .654 (2v3) | .655 (2v3) | +0.1pp |
| arXiv | OLMo-1B | 1 | 1 | .518 (1v2) | .509 (1v2) | -0.9pp |

  "content": "[Director 指令] ## Round 20 审稿修复 — 写作修改第一批（纯写作，不依赖新数据）\n\n以下修改不需要等 Agent 数据验证，立即开始。**每个修改完成后 git commit。**\n\n投稿目标：EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25。引用不需要验证（第二批会处理新引用）。\n\n---\n\n### 1. MF-1: DPI 论证修正（3 处）\n\n**(a) introduction.tex L28:**\n当前: \"The Data Processing Inequality implies that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$; its actual value is determined empirically.\"\n改为: \"The Data Processing Inequality guarantees monotonic cumulative information loss across generations; together with Markov chain convergence — adjacent transitions $Y_{k-1} \\to Y_k$ become increasingly similar as the chain approaches stationarity — this motivates a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$, whose actual value must be determined empirically.\"\n\n**(b) method.tex L9-10:** 当前已有 \"Together with Markov chain convergence\"。在 \"DPI guarantees existence but does not bound $\\Ktheory$\" 后追加一句: \"Specifically, DPI constrains the cumulative divergence from the seed text; the adjacent-pair convergence that determines $\\Kempirical$ follows from the chain's approach to its stationary distribution, where successive transition distributions become indistinguishable.\"\n\n**(c) discussion.tex L36-37:** \"Why K*≤3\" 段中，在 \"The empirical finding $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$...\" 前加: \"DPI motivates cumulative decay across the generational chain;\"\n\n### 2. SF-4: Two-tier ceiling 术语修正\n\n**不做全局替换**，只在首次出现时加定语：\n- Abstract L41: \"two-tier ceiling\" → \"feature-set-dependent two-tier structure\"\n- Discussion L29 首次出现时同样修改\n- 后续出现可保留 \"two-tier gap\" 简写\n- Discussion L29 末尾加一句: \"This gap reflects classifier capacity rather than an intrinsic domain ceiling; more expressive representations may further raise $\\Kempirical$.\"\n\n### 3. SF-6: 方法论新颖性论证\n\nIntroduction L29 \"We present an empirical characterization study\" 前插入 2-3 句：\n\"We deliberately employ simple, interpretable features to establish a conservative lower bound on the discrimination boundary: any more expressive classifier can only raise $\\Kempirical$. The DeBERTa results (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) confirm this design — neural representations push $\\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains — validating the lower-bound interpretation and demonstrating that our measurements are not artifacts of weak classifiers.\"\n\n### 4. SF-7: Conclusion 扩写\n\n当前 conclusion.tex 仅 6 行。扩写到 12-15 行，增加：\n- (1) 实用指导：binary filtering for web text/code (K*≤2 domains), finer stratification for academic text (K*=3)\n- (2) Framework 可扩展性：新 domain/model/feature set 可直接 plug in\n- (3) 与 model collapse 文献的连接：K*≤3 implies population-level quality metrics most diagnostic in first 2-3 generations\n- (4) Future directions：scale beyond 32B, mixed prompt/retrain chains, richer feature representations\n\n**注意写作规则：正文中不要出现破折号（除专有名词外）。**\n\n### 5. SF-8: 混合 chain 讨论\n\nDiscussion 末尾（L44 后）加新段落 \"Mixed-chain scenarios\"（3-4 句）:\n\"Real web-scale contamination likely involves a mixture of prompt-chain and retrain-chain processes. Our results bound the two extremes: prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ provides a text-intrinsic floor, while retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$--$7$ reflects training dynamics. The mixed-chain $\\Kempirical$ is expected to fall between these bounds, but whether prompt-chain laundering can mask retrain-chain depth signals remains an open question.\"\n\n### 6. SF-9: Scale 外推弱化\n\ndiscussion.tex L32-33: 当前 \"Log-linear extrapolation places the $\\Kempirical{=}1$ crossover at roughly 200--500B parameters, but this rests on only three scale points.\"\n改为: \"The trend suggests eventual decay toward $\\Kempirical{=}1$ at larger scales, but three data points are insufficient for reliable extrapolation; we report this as a qualitative observation rather than a quantitative prediction.\"\n\n### 7. M-3: 符号表\n\nAppendix 加 \"Notation\" 小节，列出：K*, K*_theory, K*_neural, K*_binary, θ, AUC, JSD, OBD, LOFO, PELT。每个一行描述。\n\n### 8. M-4: \"model-free\" → \"generator-independent\"\n\n全文查找 \"model-free\" 并替换为 \"generator-independent\"。注意：\n- Abstract 中如有 \"model-free\" 也要改\n- method.tex 中对 scorer model 的说明不受影响（scorer 是 external reference model，不是 generator）\n\n### 9. M-5: Bootstrap resample 数说明\n\nmethod.tex L17 脚注已有解释，加强为: \"We use 1{,}000 resamples as the default for computational efficiency; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 to achieve tighter bounds at marginal boundaries where sub-pp differences determine the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical{=}3$ verdict.\"\n\n---\n\n完成后汇报：每项修改的 diff 位置 + 编译结果。"

_source line 14664 · 2026-05-20T14:03:19.841Z_

[Director 指令] ## Round 20 审稿修复 — 写作修改第一批（纯写作，不依赖新数据）

以下修改不需要等 Agent 数据验证，立即开始。**每个修改完成后 git commit。**

投稿目标：EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25。引用不需要验证（第二批会处理新引用）。

---

### 1. MF-1: DPI 论证修正（3 处）

**(a) introduction.tex L28:**
当前: "The Data Processing Inequality implies that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$; its actual value is determined empirically."
改为: "The Data Processing Inequality guarantees monotonic cumulative information loss across generations; together with Markov chain convergence — adjacent transitions $Y_{k-1} \to Y_k$ become increasingly similar as the chain approaches stationarity — this motivates a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$, whose actual value must be determined empirically."

**(b) method.tex L9-10:** 当前已有 "Together with Markov chain convergence"。在 "DPI guarantees existence but does not bound $\Ktheory$" 后追加一句: "Specifically, DPI constrains the cumulative divergence from the seed text; the adjacent-pair convergence that determines $\Kempirical$ follows from the chain's approach to its stationary distribution, where successive transition distributions become indistinguishable."

**(c) discussion.tex L36-37:** "Why K*≤3" 段中，在 "The empirical finding $\Kempirical \leq 3$..." 前加: "DPI motivates cumulative decay across the generational chain;"

### 2. SF-4: Two-tier ceiling 术语修正

**不做全局替换**，只在首次出现时加定语：
- Abstract L41: "two-tier ceiling" → "feature-set-dependent two-tier structure"
- Discussion L29 首次出现时同样修改
- 后续出现可保留 "two-tier gap" 简写
- Discussion L29 末尾加一句: "This gap reflects classifier capacity rather than an intrinsic domain ceiling; more expressive representations may further raise $\Kempirical$."

### 3. SF-6: 方法论新颖性论证

Introduction L29 "We present an empirical characterization study" 前插入 2-3 句：
"We deliberately employ simple, interpretable features to establish a conservative lower bound on the discrimination boundary: any more expressive classifier can only raise $\Kempirical$. The DeBERTa results (Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}) confirm this design — neural representations push $\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains — validating the lower-bound interpretation and demonstrating that our measurements are not artifacts of weak classifiers."

### 4. SF-7: Conclusion 扩写

当前 conclusion.tex 仅 6 行。扩写到 12-15 行，增加：
- (1) 实用指导：binary filtering for web text/code (K*≤2 domains), finer stratification for academic text (K*=3)
- (2) Framework 可扩展性：新 domain/model/feature set 可直接 plug in
- (3) 与 model collapse 文献的连接：K*≤3 implies population-level quality metrics most diagnostic in first 2-3 generations
- (4) Future directions：scale beyond 32B, mixed prompt/retrain chains, richer feature representations

**注意写作规则：正文中不要出现破折号（除专有名词外）。**

### 5. SF-8: 混合 chain 讨论

Discussion 末尾（L44 后）加新段落 "Mixed-chain scenarios"（3-4 句）:
"Real web-scale contamination likely involves a mixture of prompt-chain and retrain-chain processes. Our results bound the two extremes: prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$ provides a text-intrinsic floor, while retrain-chain $\Kempirical{\geq}4$--$7$ reflects training dynamics. The mixed-chain $\Kempirical$ is expected to fall between these bounds, but whether prompt-chain laundering can mask retrain-chain depth signals remains an open question."

### 6. SF-9: Scale 外推弱化

discussion.tex L32-33: 当前 "Log-linear extrapolation places the $\Kempirical{=}1$ crossover at roughly 200--500B parameters, but this rests on only three scale points."
改为: "The trend suggests eventual decay toward $\Kempirical{=}1$ at larger scales, but three data points are insufficient for reliable extrapolation; we report this as a qualitative observation rather than a quantitative prediction."

### 7. M-3: 符号表

Appendix 加 "Notation" 小节，列出：K*, K*_theory, K*_neural, K*_binary, θ, AUC, JSD, OBD, LOFO, PELT。每个一行描述。

### 8. M-4: "model-free" → "generator-independent"

全文查找 "model-free" 并替换为 "generator-independent"。注意：
- Abstract 中如有 "model-free" 也要改
- method.tex 中对 scorer model 的说明不受影响（scorer 是 external reference model，不是 generator）

### 9. M-5: Bootstrap resample 数说明

method.tex L17 脚注已有解释，加强为: "We use 1{,}000 resamples as the default for computational efficiency; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 to achieve tighter bounds at marginal boundaries where sub-pp differences determine the $\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\ $\Kempirical{=}3$ verdict."

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Under prompt-chain recursion ($\leq$32B), the $\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across 7/9 continuation-mode configurations (4/6 families); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated ceilings across all four domains.
5	DeBERTa matches OBD on low-complexity domains but extends $\Kempirical$ by $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (two-tier ceiling).
6	$\Kempirical$ localizes where depth-aware curation yields diminishing returns; retrain-chain experiments confirm $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ across three families, and scaling beyond 32B remains open.
7	
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QsYeKvzWg8BWtci1TYn2TJ`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
5	\label{sec:formulation}
6	
7	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
8	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
9	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.
10	Together with Markov chain convergence, this motivates a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI guarantees existence but does not bound $\Ktheory$, so the actual saturation depth must be measured empirically.
11	
12	We define the operational lower bound $K^*_{\text{empirical}}$ (hereafter $\Kempirical$):
13	\begin{equation}
14	  \Kempirical = \max\!\bigl\{k \!\geq\! 1 : \mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{j\text{-}1,j}) > \theta,\ \forall j \!\leq\! k\bigr\},
15	  \label{eq:kstar}
16	\end{equation}
17	where $\mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).
18	This criterion ensures that $\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise; the $\forall j \leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\auc_{k,k+1}$ decreases monotonically for all pairs above $\theta$ across all tested configurations; negligible fluctuations ($\Delta < 0.02$) occur only at deeper depths where discrimination approaches chance level (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).
19	Since 0v1 $\auc > \theta$ in all tested configurations, $\Kempirical \geq 1$; $\Kempirical{=}0$ (undetectable first-generation text) is not observed.
20	$\Kempirical$ is an operational quantity jointly conditioned on the feature set and threshold $\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}), though absolute $\Kempirical$ values shift with $\theta$.
21	We additionally verify that non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
22	
23	\paragraph{Threshold choice.}
24	We set $\theta{=}0.60$ as a post-hoc operational threshold, chosen at the empirical discontinuity in the AUC distribution (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).
25	A 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\,0.55$ and $p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons, placing $\theta{=}0.60$ well above the empirical null.
26	$\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\theta \in [0.55, 0.65]$ for all but two marginal configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}): 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\sim}$5--11\,pp discontinuity.
27	Only at extremes does $\Kempirical$ shift: $\theta{=}0.55$ yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\theta{=}0.70$ yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ across all configurations.
28	The downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\theta$, avoiding circularity.
29	A leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation on C4 provides an internal consistency check (not external validation): each of five folds derives $\hat{\theta}$ from the gap midpoint between $\min(\auc_{1v2})$ and $\max(\auc_{2v3})$ over the remaining families, yielding $\hat{\theta} \in [0.5725, 0.6004]$; $\theta{=}0.60$ falls within this range, and 8/9 held-out predictions match the original $\Kempirical$ (the single mismatch is the borderline rewrite-mode configuration; Table~\ref{tab:theta_lofo}).
30	ArXiv corroborates with 6/6 LOFO agreement; Wikipedia is underdetermined because most models produce near-chance 1v2 AUC, invalidating the gap-based derivation (Appendix~\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).
31	
32	
33	\subsection{Features and Classifier}
34	\label{sec:features}
35	
36	Each document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).
37	All features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).
38	Self-BLEU is computed within each CV fold using only same-fold, same-depth reference samples to prevent leakage; it ranks \#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.
39	Removing self-BLEU reduces mean $\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\,pp across all models (14D $\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations; bootstrap CIs in Table~\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}); in deployment where depth labels are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted with minimal performance loss.
40	Hand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer without retraining and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.
41	
42	We use \emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\text{depth} > k \mid \mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM~\cite{ke2017lightgbm} (hyperparameters in Table~\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).
43	OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).
44	
45	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
46	\label{sec:experimental_setup}
47	
48	We evaluate ten configurations spanning six architecture families (Table~\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation modes), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base, continuation), Qwen2.5-7B, Qwen2.5-14B, Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Gemma-2-2B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B (all continuation except Qwen-rw).
49	Each uses 5{,}000 C4~\cite{raffel2020t5} seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).
50	Default decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.
51	Continuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.
52	Evaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\auc$ as primary metric.
53	
54	\begin{table}[t]
55	\centering
56	\small
57	\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}
58	\label{tab:setup}
59	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
60	\begin{tabular}{llcc}
61	\toprule
62	\textbf{Config} & \textbf{Model} & \textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\
63	\midrule
64	Qwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\
65	Qwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
66	Qwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
67	Pythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
68	OLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
69	Gemma     & Gemma-2-2B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
70	Qwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
71	Qwen-14B  & Qwen2.5-14B           & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
72	LLaMA-8B  & LLaMA-3.1-8B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
73	Mistral   & Mistral-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
74	\bottomrule
75	\end{tabular}}
76	\end{table}
77	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.
5	Estimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.
6	When synthetic text is recursively fed back as input, the resulting multi-generational content degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.
7	This degradation arises in both \emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \emph{prompt-chains} (recursively prompting a fixed model to generate from its own prior outputs); we study prompt-chains, which isolate text-intrinsic information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
8	Yet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.
9	
10	\begin{figure*}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}
13	\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}
14	\label{fig:pipeline}
15	\end{figure*}
16	
17	This gap cuts across multiple communities.
18	Binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).
19	Membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.
20	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.
21	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
22	
23	Knowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.
24	For data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principled choice.
25	Model collapse theory predicts monotonic quality degradation across generations, but existing analyses are population-level and provide no per-text measure of where degradation saturates.
26	$\Kempirical$ fills this gap by localizing the generation at which adjacent outputs become empirically indistinguishable.
27	
28	The Data Processing Inequality implies that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$; its actual value is determined empirically.
29	We present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.
30	We operationalize $\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \geq 1$ where the pairwise $\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\ref{sec:formulation}).
31	
32	Our contributions:
33	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
34	  \item \textbf{First empirical measurement of $\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$: 4/6 families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 ($p \leq 0.002$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.008$ $\auc$ for generator identity).
35	  Zero-shot frontier LLMs likewise collapse beyond $\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\% (vs.\ 20\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Section~\ref{sec:why_kstar}).
36	  \item \textbf{Domain ordering}: arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across tested configurations; code (Python) matches web text depth despite a different modality, confirmed by multi-source pooling (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
37	  \item \textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-independent features within continuation mode enable 80--92\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\rho > 0.90$; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\Kempirical$ assignments are fragile under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$); as a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\Kempirical$ correctly predicts binary ${>}$ graduated filtering for web text (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
38	\end{enumerate}
39	
```

_source line 14673 · 2026-05-20T14:03:39.195Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Scocr2fdFnDBceJfbnZxjs`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.
19	Several configurations are near-boundary (CI margins 0.2--4.5\,pp above $\theta$), and cross-model transfer at 7B preserves aggregate accuracy (96--100\% pairwise AUC retention) but not boundary-level assignments (4/6 off-diagonal pairs fall below $\theta$), underscoring that $\Kempirical{=}2$ is a robust population-level finding but fragile at the per-configuration level.
20	
21	\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain.}
22	All main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.
23	Retrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (3v4 $\auc = 0.682$; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), confirming across three architecture families that retrain-chain $\Kempirical$ is protocol-determined rather than architecture-specific.
24	Prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$ is thus the operationally relevant floor for data curation; retrain-chain $\Kempirical{=}7$ the upper bound under controlled conditions.
25	
26	\paragraph{JSD decay, domain dependence, and the two-tier ceiling.}
27	Token-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$, $\Kempirical{=}7$; Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
28	The domain ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}): domains with richer formatting conventions (LaTeX markup, citation patterns, mathematical notation) retain more depth-distinguishing signal per generation.
29	The classifier gap confirms the two-tier ceiling: DeBERTa $=$ OBD on C4 and Code but $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}).
30	
31	\paragraph{Scale trends ($\leq$32B).}
32	LightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.621 (14B) to 0.618 (32B), a total drop of 0.8\,pp (${\sim}$0.4\,pp per 10$\times$ scale increase).
33	Log-linear extrapolation places the $\Kempirical{=}1$ crossover at roughly 200--500B parameters, but this rests on only three scale points.
34	
35	\paragraph{Why $\Kempirical \leq 3$ under model-free features.}
36	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees that $\Ktheory$ is finite but provides no upper bound.
37	The empirical finding $\Kempirical \leq 3$ across all domains indicates that 15-dimensional statistical features exhaust depth-distinguishing information within two to three generations.
38	This is consistent with the JSD cliff on C4 ($\lambda \approx 2.9$; Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): token-level divergence decays exponentially, leaving insufficient signal for the OBD classifier beyond the first few boundaries.
39	Richer feature sets or learned representations may push $\Kempirical$ higher, as the DeBERTa two-tier results suggest.
40	
41	\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}
42	Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains, indicating that $\Kempirical$ is an intrinsic property of the domain rather than of any particular generator.
43	For corpus curation, filtering strategies can be calibrated by domain type alone: binary for web text and code ($\Kempirical{=}2$), finer stratification for academic text ($\Kempirical{=}3$).
44	For the model collapse community, $\Kempirical \leq 3$ implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations.
45	
46	
```

    "oldString": "The Data Processing Inequality implies that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$; its actual value is determined empirically.\nWe present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\\Kempirical$",
    "newString": "The Data Processing Inequality guarantees monotonic cumulative information loss across generations; together with Markov chain convergence (adjacent transitions $Y_{k-1} \\to Y_k$ become increasingly similar as the chain approaches stationarity), this motivates a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$, whose actual value must be determined empirically.\nWe deliberately employ simple, interpretable features to establish a conservative lower bound on the discrimination boundary: any more expressive classifier can only raise $\\Kempirical$.\nThe DeBERTa results (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) confirm this design: neural representations push $\\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains, validating the lower-bound interpretation and demonstrating that our measurements are not artifacts of weak classifiers.\nWe present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\\Kempirical$",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen synthetic text is recursively fed back as input, the resulting multi-generational content degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively prompting a fixed model to generate from its own prior outputs); we study prompt-chains, which isolate text-intrinsic information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nThis gap cuts across multiple communities.\nBinary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).\nMembership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.\nData selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.\n\nKnowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.\nFor data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principled choice.\nModel collapse theory predicts monotonic quality degradation across generations, but existing analyses are population-level and provide no per-text measure of where degradation saturates.\n$\\Kempirical$ fills this gap by localizing the generation at which adjacent outputs become empirically indistinguishable.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality implies that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$; its actual value is determined empirically.\nWe present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{First empirical measurement of $\\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\\theta{=}0.60$: 4/6 families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.008$ $\\auc$ for generator identity).\n  Zero-shot frontier LLMs likewise collapse beyond $\\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\\% (vs.\\ 20\\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Domain ordering}: arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across tested configurations; code (Python) matches web text depth despite a different modality, confirmed by multi-source pooling (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-independent features within continuation mode enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments are fragile under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$); as a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts binary ${>}$ graduated filtering for web text (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

    "newString": "The classifier gap confirms a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure: DeBERTa $=$ OBD on C4 and Code but $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}).\nThis gap reflects classifier capacity rather than an intrinsic domain ceiling; more expressive representations may further raise $\\Kempirical$.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.\nSeveral configurations are near-boundary (CI margins 0.2--4.5\\,pp above $\\theta$), and cross-model transfer at 7B preserves aggregate accuracy (96--100\\% pairwise AUC retention) but not boundary-level assignments (4/6 off-diagonal pairs fall below $\\theta$), underscoring that $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is a robust population-level finding but fragile at the per-configuration level.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (3v4 $\\auc = 0.682$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), confirming across three architecture families that retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ is protocol-determined rather than architecture-specific.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is thus the operationally relevant floor for data curation; retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ the upper bound under controlled conditions.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay, domain dependence, and the two-tier ceiling.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$, $\\Kempirical{=}7$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\nThe domain ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}): domains with richer formatting conventions (LaTeX markup, citation patterns, mathematical notation) retain more depth-distinguishing signal per generation.\nThe classifier gap confirms the two-tier ceiling: DeBERTa $=$ OBD on C4 and Code but $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}).\n\n\\paragraph{Scale trends ($\\leq$32B).}\nLightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.621 (14B) to 0.618 (32B), a total drop of 0.8\\,pp (${\\sim}$0.4\\,pp per 10$\\times$ scale increase).\nLog-linear extrapolation places the $\\Kempirical{=}1$ crossover at roughly 200--500B parameters, but this rests on only three scale points.\n\n\\paragraph{Why $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ under model-free features.}\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that $\\Ktheory$ is finite but provides no upper bound.\nThe empirical finding $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ across all domains indicates that 15-dimensional statistical features exhaust depth-distinguishing information within two to three generations.\nThis is consistent with the JSD cliff on C4 ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$; Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): token-level divergence decays exponentially, leaving insufficient signal for the OBD classifier beyond the first few boundaries.\nRicher feature sets or learned representations may push $\\Kempirical$ higher, as the DeBERTa two-tier results suggest.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, indicating that $\\Kempirical$ is an intrinsic property of the domain rather than of any particular generator.\nFor corpus curation, filtering strategies can be calibrated by domain type alone: binary for web text and code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), finer stratification for academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$).\nFor the model collapse community, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations.\n\n",

C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.\nSeveral configurations are near-boundary (CI margins 0.2--4.5\\,pp above $\\theta$), and cross-model transfer at 7B preserves aggregate accuracy (96--100\\% pairwise AUC retention) but not boundary-level assignments (4/6 off-diagonal pairs fall below $\\theta$), underscoring that $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is a robust population-level finding but fragile at the per-configuration level.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (3v4 $\\auc = 0.682$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), confirming across three architecture families that retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ is protocol-determined rather than architecture-specific.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is thus the operationally relevant floor for data curation; retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ the upper bound under controlled conditions.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay, domain dependence, and the two-tier ceiling.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$, $\\Kempirical{=}7$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\nThe domain ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}): domains with richer formatting conventions (LaTeX markup, citation patterns, mathematical notation) retain more depth-distinguishing signal per generation.\nThe classifier gap confirms a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure: DeBERTa $=$ OBD on C4 and Code but $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}).\nThis gap reflects classifier capacity rather than an intrinsic domain ceiling; more expressive representations may further raise $\\Kempirical$.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale trends ($\\leq$32B).}\nLightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.621 (14B) to 0.618 (32B), a total drop of 0.8\\,pp (${\\sim}$0.4\\,pp per 10$\\times$ scale increase).\nThe trend suggests eventual decay toward $\\Kempirical{=}1$ at larger scales, but three data points are insufficient for reliable extrapolation; we report this as a qualitative observation rather than a quantitative prediction.\n\n\\paragraph{Why $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ under model-free features.}\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that $\\Ktheory$ is finite but provides no upper bound.\nThe empirical finding $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ across all domains indicates that 15-dimensional statistical features exhaust depth-distinguishing information within two to three generations.\nThis is consistent with the JSD cliff on C4 ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$; Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): token-level divergence decays exponentially, leaving insufficient signal for the OBD classifier beyond the first few boundaries.\nRicher feature sets or learned representations may push $\\Kempirical$ higher, as the DeBERTa two-tier results suggest.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, indicating that $\\Kempirical$ is an intrinsic property of the domain rather than of any particular generator.\nFor corpus curation, filtering strategies can be calibrated by domain type alone: binary for web text and code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), finer stratification for academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$).\nFor the model collapse community, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations.\n\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this motivates a finite $\\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI guarantees existence but does not bound $\\Ktheory$, so the actual saturation depth must be measured empirically.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound $K^*_{\\text{empirical}}$ (hereafter $\\Kempirical$):\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\!\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{j\\text{-}1,j}) > \\theta,\\ \\forall j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).\nThis criterion ensures that $\\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise; the $\\forall j \\leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k,k+1}$ decreases monotonically for all pairs above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations; negligible fluctuations ($\\Delta < 0.02$) occur only at deeper depths where discrimination approaches chance level (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nSince 0v1 $\\auc > \\theta$ in all tested configurations, $\\Kempirical \\geq 1$; $\\Kempirical{=}0$ (undetectable first-generation text) is not observed.\n$\\Kempirical$ is an operational quantity jointly conditioned on the feature set and threshold $\\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}), though absolute $\\Kempirical$ values shift with $\\theta$.\nWe additionally verify that non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold choice.}\nWe set $\\theta{=}0.60$ as a post-hoc operational threshold, chosen at the empirical discontinuity in the AUC distribution (Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\nA 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ and $p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons, placing $\\theta{=}0.60$ well above the empirical null.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.55, 0.65]$ for all but two marginal configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}): 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity.\nOnly at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift: $\\theta{=}0.55$ yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$ yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all configurations.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\nA leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation on C4 provides an internal consistency check (not external validation): each of five folds derives $\\hat{\\theta}$ from the gap midpoint between $\\min(\\auc_{1v2})$ and $\\max(\\auc_{2v3})$ over the remaining families, yielding $\\hat{\\theta} \\in [0.5725, 0.6004]$; $\\theta{=}0.60$ falls within this range, and 8/9 held-out predictions match the original $\\Kempirical$ (the single mismatch is the borderline rewrite-mode configuration; Table~\\ref{tab:theta_lofo}).\nArXiv corroborates with 6/6 LOFO agreement; Wikipedia is underdetermined because most models produce near-chance 1v2 AUC, invalidating the gap-based derivation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nSelf-BLEU is computed within each CV fold using only same-fold, same-depth reference samples to prevent leakage; it ranks \\#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.\nRemoving self-BLEU reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\\,pp across all models (14D $\\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations; bootstrap CIs in Table~\\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}); in deployment where depth labels are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted with minimal performance loss.\nHand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer without retraining and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM~\\cite{ke2017lightgbm} (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate ten configurations spanning six architecture families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation modes), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base, continuation), Qwen2.5-7B, Qwen2.5-14B, Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Gemma-2-2B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B (all continuation except Qwen-rw).\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4~\\cite{raffel2020t5} seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nGemma     & Gemma-2-2B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-14B  & Qwen2.5-14B           & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B  & LLaMA-3.1-8B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nMistral   & Mistral-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n",

          "-where $\\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).",
          "+where $\\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\\footnote{We use 1{,}000 resamples as the default for computational efficiency; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 to achieve tighter bounds at marginal boundaries where sub-pp differences determine the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical{=}3$ verdict.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).",
          " This criterion ensures that $\\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise; the $\\forall j \\leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k,k+1}$ decreases monotonically for all pairs above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations; negligible fluctuations ($\\Delta < 0.02$) occur only at deeper depths where discrimination approaches chance level (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).",
          " Since 0v1 $\\auc > \\theta$ in all tested configurations, $\\Kempirical \\geq 1$; $\\Kempirical{=}0$ (undetectable first-generation text) is not observed.",
          " $\\Kempirical$ is an operational quantity jointly conditioned on the feature set and threshold $\\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}), though absolute $\\Kempirical$ values shift with $\\theta$."
        ]
      }

    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main - ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage{pifont}\n\\newcommand{\\cmark}{\\ding{51}}\n\\newcommand{\\xmark}{\\ding{55}}\n\\usepackage{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\\newcommand{\\tpm}{\\ensuremath{\\,\\pm\\,}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using model-free statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across models ($\\leq$32B); within this hierarchy, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text emerges as the dominant mode (7/9 continuation-mode configurations, four of six families).\nA fine-tuned DeBERTa classifier cannot break this ceiling on low-complexity domains (C4, Code) but extends $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (two-tier ceiling).\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\\% accuracy) but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ is fragile.\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 10-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (6 architecture families; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B, Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$); a $\\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5 of 10 total configurations (4 of 9 continuation-mode); though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the five single-configuration families.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

  "new_string": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nUnder prompt-chain recursion ($\\leq$32B), the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across 7/9 continuation-mode configurations (4/6 families); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated ceilings across all four domains.\nDeBERTa matches OBD on low-complexity domains but extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv, establishing a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure.\nRetrain-chain experiments confirm $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ across three architecture families, placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the text-intrinsic floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the protocol-dependent upper bound.\n\nThese results yield concrete guidance for corpus curation: binary filtering (remove all $\\text{depth} \\geq 1$) is the principled choice for web text and code ($\\Kempirical \\leq 2$), while academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) may benefit from finer depth stratification.\n$\\Kempirical$ localizes where depth-aware filtering yields diminishing returns, preventing practitioners from investing in graduated schemes beyond the discrimination boundary.\nThe framework is extensible: new domains, models, or feature sets can be evaluated by rerunning the OBD pipeline without architectural changes.\n\nFor the model collapse community, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ under generator-independent features implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations; monitoring beyond this point is unlikely to reveal additional degradation structure.\nOpen directions include scaling beyond 32B (where the trend toward $\\Kempirical{=}1$ may accelerate), characterizing mixed prompt/retrain chains that better approximate real-world contamination, and exploring richer feature representations that may push $\\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains."
}

    "oldString": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nUnder prompt-chain recursion ($\\leq$32B), the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across 7/9 continuation-mode configurations (4/6 families); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated ceilings across all four domains.\nDeBERTa matches OBD on low-complexity domains but extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (two-tier ceiling).\n$\\Kempirical$ localizes where depth-aware curation yields diminishing returns; retrain-chain experiments confirm $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ across three families, and scaling beyond 32B remains open.",
    "newString": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nUnder prompt-chain recursion ($\\leq$32B), the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across 7/9 continuation-mode configurations (4/6 families); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated ceilings across all four domains.\nDeBERTa matches OBD on low-complexity domains but extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv, establishing a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure.\nRetrain-chain experiments confirm $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ across three architecture families, placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the text-intrinsic floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the protocol-dependent upper bound.\n\nThese results yield concrete guidance for corpus curation: binary filtering (remove all $\\text{depth} \\geq 1$) is the principled choice for web text and code ($\\Kempirical \\leq 2$), while academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) may benefit from finer depth stratification.\n$\\Kempirical$ localizes where depth-aware filtering yields diminishing returns, preventing practitioners from investing in graduated schemes beyond the discrimination boundary.\nThe framework is extensible: new domains, models, or feature sets can be evaluated by rerunning the OBD pipeline without architectural changes.\n\nFor the model collapse community, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ under generator-independent features implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations; monitoring beyond this point is unlikely to reveal additional degradation structure.\nOpen directions include scaling beyond 32B (where the trend toward $\\Kempirical{=}1$ may accelerate), characterizing mixed prompt/retrain chains that better approximate real-world contamination, and exploring richer feature representations that may push $\\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains.",
