    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main - ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage{pifont}\n\\newcommand{\\cmark}{\\ding{51}}\n\\newcommand{\\xmark}{\\ding{55}}\n\\usepackage{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\\newcommand{\\tpm}{\\ensuremath{\\,\\pm\\,}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using model-free statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across models ($\\leq$32B); within this hierarchy, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text emerges as the dominant mode (7/9 continuation-mode configurations, four of six families).\nA fine-tuned DeBERTa classifier cannot break this ceiling on low-complexity domains (C4, Code) but extends $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (feature-set-dependent two-tier structure).\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\\% accuracy) but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ is fragile.\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 10-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (6 architecture families; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B, Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$); a $\\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5 of 10 total configurations (4 of 9 continuation-mode); though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the five single-configuration families.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.\nSeveral configurations are near-boundary (CI margins 0.2--4.5\\,pp above $\\theta$), and cross-model transfer at 7B preserves aggregate accuracy (96--100\\% pairwise AUC retention) but not boundary-level assignments (4/6 off-diagonal pairs fall below $\\theta$), underscoring that $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is a robust population-level finding but fragile at the per-configuration level.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (3v4 $\\auc = 0.682$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), confirming across three architecture families that retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ is protocol-determined rather than architecture-specific.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is thus the operationally relevant floor for data curation; retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ the upper bound under controlled conditions.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay, domain dependence, and the two-tier ceiling.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$, $\\Kempirical{=}7$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\nThe domain ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}): domains with richer formatting conventions (LaTeX markup, citation patterns, mathematical notation) retain more depth-distinguishing signal per generation.\nThe classifier gap confirms a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure: DeBERTa $=$ OBD on C4 and Code but $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}).\nThis gap reflects classifier capacity rather than an intrinsic domain ceiling; more expressive representations may further raise $\\Kempirical$.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale trends ($\\leq$32B).}\nLightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.621 (14B) to 0.618 (32B), a total drop of 0.8\\,pp (${\\sim}$0.4\\,pp per 10$\\times$ scale increase).\nThe trend suggests eventual decay toward $\\Kempirical{=}1$ at larger scales, but three data points are insufficient for reliable extrapolation; we report this as a qualitative observation rather than a quantitative prediction.\n\n\\paragraph{Why $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ under model-free features.}\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that $\\Ktheory$ is finite but provides no upper bound.\nDPI motivates cumulative decay across the generational chain; the empirical finding $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ across all domains indicates that 15-dimensional statistical features exhaust depth-distinguishing information within two to three generations.\nThis is consistent with the JSD cliff on C4 ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$; Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): token-level divergence decays exponentially, leaving insufficient signal for the OBD classifier beyond the first few boundaries.\nRicher feature sets or learned representations may push $\\Kempirical$ higher, as the DeBERTa two-tier results suggest.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, indicating that $\\Kempirical$ is an intrinsic property of the domain rather than of any particular generator.\nFor corpus curation, filtering strategies can be calibrated by domain type alone: binary for web text and code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), finer stratification for academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$).\nFor the model collapse community, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal web-scale contamination likely involves a mixture of prompt-chain and retrain-chain processes.\nOur results bound the two extremes: prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ provides a text-intrinsic floor, while retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$--$7$ reflects training dynamics.\nThe mixed-chain $\\Kempirical$ is expected to fall between these bounds, but whether prompt-chain laundering can mask retrain-chain depth signals remains an open question.\n\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01RXoDQ4p6jc8DFzkxnCiRvd`</summary>

```text
17	This gap cuts across multiple communities.
18	Binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).
19	Membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.
20	Data selection methods~\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.
21	No existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
22	
23	Knowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.
24	For data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principled choice.
25	Model collapse theory predicts monotonic quality degradation across generations, but existing analyses are population-level and provide no per-text measure of where degradation saturates.
26	$\Kempirical$ fills this gap by localizing the generation at which adjacent outputs become empirically indistinguishable.
27	
28	The Data Processing Inequality guarantees monotonic cumulative information loss across generations; together with Markov chain convergence (adjacent transitions $Y_{k-1} \to Y_k$ become increasingly similar as the chain approaches stationarity), this motivates a finite discrimination boundary $\Kstar$, whose actual value must be determined empirically.
29	We deliberately employ simple, interpretable features to establish a conservative lower bound on the discrimination boundary: any more expressive classifier can only raise $\Kempirical$.
30	The DeBERTa results (Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}) confirm this design: neural representations push $\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains, validating the lower-bound interpretation and demonstrating that our measurements are not artifacts of weak classifiers.
31	We present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.
```

```text
23	We additionally verify that non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
24	
25	\paragraph{Threshold choice.}
26	We set $\theta{=}0.60$ as a post-hoc operational threshold, chosen at the empirical discontinuity in the AUC distribution (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).
27	A 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\,0.55$ and $p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons, placing $\theta{=}0.60$ well above the empirical null.
28	$\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\theta \in [0.55, 0.65]$ for all but two marginal configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}): 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\sim}$5--11\,pp discontinuity.
29	Only at extremes does $\Kempirical$ shift: $\theta{=}0.55$ yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\theta{=}0.70$ yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ across all configurations.
30	The downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\theta$, avoiding circularity.
31	A leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation on C4 provides an internal consistency check (not external validation): each of five folds derives $\hat{\theta}$ from the gap midpoint between $\min(\auc_{1v2})$ and $\max(\auc_{2v3})$ over the remaining families, yielding $\hat{\theta} \in [0.5725, 0.6004]$; $\theta{=}0.60$ falls within this range, and 8/9 held-out predictions match the original $\Kempirical$ (the single mismatch is the borderline rewrite-mode configuration; Table~\ref{tab:theta_lofo}).
32	ArXiv corroborates with 6/6 LOFO agreement; Wikipedia is underdetermined because most models produce near-chance 1v2 AUC, invalidating the gap-based derivation (Appendix~\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).
```

**Introduction gap (L17-21):**
Old:
```
This gap cuts across multiple communities.
Binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).


```

New:
```
This gap cuts across multiple communities: binary detectors~\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate, membership inference~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and model collapse literature provides no per-text tool. Data selection methods likewise ignore recursive depth, leaving no existing approach to estimate how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.
```

Now for the practical implications: knowing where discrimination saturates has direct consequences for data curation—if the saturation point is depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that becomes noise, making binary filtering the principled choice. Model collapse theory predicts monotonic degradation across generations, but existing analyses operate at population level without per-text measures of where that degradation actually saturates. I'm introducing an empirical method that localizes the generation at which adjacent outputs become indistinguishable. I'm setting the threshold at 0.60 based on where the AUC distribution shows an empirical discontinuity, and a permutation test confirms this sits well above the noise floor. The key finding is that $\Kempirical{=}2$ holds stable across a reasonable range of threshold values, though there's a notable gap between the 1v2 and 2v3 comparisons that only breaks down at the extremes.

To validate this choice, I'm running a leave-one-family-out cross-validation where each fold independently derives its own threshold from the gap between the two comparison groups, and the resulting estimates cluster tightly around 0.60, with the original threshold falling squarely in that range and recovering the same $\Kempirical$ value in nearly all held-out cases. I'm checking the robustness of this threshold choice across a range of values—$K_{\text{empirical}}$ stays at 2 across the interval [0.55, 0.65], only shifting at the extremes, and the leave-one-family-out validation on C4 confirms the threshold estimate with strong agreement across held-out families and the arXiv dataset.

For the arXiv experiments, all five models consistently identify $K_{\text{empirical}} = 3$, with the boundary-pair AUC scores all exceeding the threshold, suggesting that the stronger distributional shift in arXiv—driven by formulaic features like LaTeX and citations that persist through generation—provides clearer depth signals across different architectures.

When I remove LaTeX markup, the 2v3 AUC drops by about 1.8 percentage points but $K_{\text{empirical}}$ stays at 3, indicating that content-level properties are what really matter. OLMo-1B shows near-total information loss rather than a genuine counter-example, but Mistral-7B is particularly revealing: it achieves $K_{\text{empirical}} = 1$ on C4 yet $K_{\text{empirical}} = 3$ on arXiv, demonstrating that domain structure alone can shift the complexity level by two tiers within the same model. Mistral-7B shows the clearest domain dependence—it achieves $\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4 but jumps to $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, demonstrating that domain structure alone can shift the empirical $K$ by two levels.

For the feature representation, I'm using a 15-dimensional vector that captures four aspects of token-level surprisal: distributional moments (mean, standard deviation, skewness, kurtosis), local dynamics (first and second-order differences with their statistics), vocabulary richness (type-token ratio, hapax legomena, self-BLEU), and tail behavior (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction).

Self-BLEU ranks highly in greedy decoding but drops out of the top-3 for beam search variants, and ablating it costs less than 1 percentage point in AUC across all models. In practical settings where depth labels aren't available, I can substitute corpus-level self-similarity or remove it entirely with negligible performance degradation. These hand-crafted features have the advantage of enabling transfer across different text generators without retraining. The self-BLEU feature has minimal impact on performance, so the hand-crafted features work well across different generators without needing retraining. I'm setting up the experimental evaluation across ten model configurations from six different architecture families, each tested in continuation mode with varying parameter sizes. We're testing ten configurations across six architecture families, each using 5,000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens and recursively generated for five iterations to create 30,000 documents per setup. For decoding, we use nucleus sampling with p=0.95 and temperature 1.0 by default, with ablations testing greedy decoding and temperatures of 0.7 and 1.2. In continuation mode, seeds are shortened to 128 tokens then extended back to 256, while rewrite mode paraphrases the entire text. We evaluate using 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document, with pairwise AUC as our primary metric.

Now I'm tallying up the compression savings across different sections—the introduction gaps, method thresholds, features, experimental setup, and arXiv experiments should collectively save around 14 to 17 lines when combined with some conservative estimates. That should give us enough room to work with. Let me start applying these compressions.

    "oldString": "This gap cuts across multiple communities.\nBinary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).\nMembership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.\nData selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.",
    "newString": "This gap cuts across multiple communities: binary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}), membership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and data selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen synthetic text is recursively fed back as input, the resulting multi-generational content degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively prompting a fixed model to generate from its own prior outputs); we study prompt-chains, which isolate text-intrinsic information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nThis gap cuts across multiple communities.\nBinary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).\nMembership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.\nData selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.\n\nKnowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.\nFor data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principled choice.\nModel collapse theory predicts monotonic quality degradation across generations, but existing analyses are population-level and provide no per-text measure of where degradation saturates.\n$\\Kempirical$ fills this gap by localizing the generation at which adjacent outputs become empirically indistinguishable.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees monotonic cumulative information loss across generations; together with Markov chain convergence (adjacent transitions $Y_{k-1} \\to Y_k$ become increasingly similar as the chain approaches stationarity), this motivates a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$, whose actual value must be determined empirically.\nWe deliberately employ simple, interpretable features to establish a conservative lower bound on the discrimination boundary: any more expressive classifier can only raise $\\Kempirical$.\nThe DeBERTa results (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) confirm this design: neural representations push $\\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains, validating the lower-bound interpretation and demonstrating that our measurements are not artifacts of weak classifiers.\nWe present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{First empirical measurement of $\\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\\theta{=}0.60$: 4/6 families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.008$ $\\auc$ for generator identity).\n  Zero-shot frontier LLMs likewise collapse beyond $\\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\\% (vs.\\ 20\\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Domain ordering}: arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across tested configurations; code (Python) matches web text depth despite a different modality, confirmed by multi-source pooling (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-independent features within continuation mode enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments are fragile under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$); as a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts binary ${>}$ graduated filtering for web text (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

Director requested git commit after each modification.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - K* (discrimination boundary) = max depth where AUC > θ=0.60
   - Domain ordering: arXiv(K*=3) > C4(K*=2) = Code(K*=2) > Wikipedia(K*=1)
   - DPI (Data Processing Inequality): guarantees monotonic cumulative information loss; Markov chain convergence motivates finite K*
   - Feature-set-dependent two-tier structure (renamed from "two-tier ceiling"): DeBERTa = OBD on C4/Code, +1 on Wikipedia/arXiv
   - Prompt-chain K*=2 (text-intrinsic) vs retrain-chain K*≥4–7 (protocol-determined, 3 families)
   - Generator-independent features (renamed from "model-free"): 15D statistical features
   - Mixed-chain scenarios: real contamination between prompt-chain floor and retrain-chain upper bound
   - EMNLP 2026: strict 8-page main body limit (Limitations/Ethics/References/Appendix unlimited)
   - Writing rule: no dashes in prose (except proper nouns), Paper Agent uses paper_agent_post_to_director

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **introduction.tex** (`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/introduction.tex`)

   - **method.tex** (`docs/paper/method.tex`)

     - L17 footnote: Expanded bootstrap explanation: "We use 1,000 resamples as the default for computational efficiency; arXiv cross-scorer CIs use 10,000 to achieve tighter bounds at marginal boundaries where sub-pp differences determine the K*=2 vs. K*=3 verdict."
     - **Planned but not yet done**: Compress threshold choice (L25-32) from ~8 to ~5 lines, compress features paragraph (L36-40), compress experimental setup (L48-52)

   - **discussion.tex** (`docs/paper/discussion.tex`)
     - Current 8 paragraphs: One-wash laundering / Prompt-chain vs retrain-chain (with OLMo K*≥4) / JSD decay+two-tier (terminology updated + new sentence) / Scale trends (extrapolation weakened) / Why K*≤3 (heading: "generator-independent features", DPI clause added) / Multi-source invariance / Mixed-chain scenarios (NEW) / [empty line at end]

   - **conclusion.tex** (`docs/paper/conclusion.tex`)
     - Expanded from 3 sentences (~6 lines) to ~15 lines:
       ```
       Under prompt-chain recursion (≤32B), the θ-invariant domain ordering arXiv (K*=3) > C4 (K*=2) = Code (K*=2) > Wikipedia (K*=1) holds across 7/9 continuation-mode configurations (4/6 families); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated ceilings across all four domains.
       DeBERTa matches OBD on low-complexity domains but extends K* by +1 on Wikipedia and arXiv, establishing a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure.
       Retrain-chain experiments confirm K*≥4 across three architecture families, placing prompt-chain K*=2 as the text-intrinsic floor and retrain-chain K*=7 as the protocol-dependent upper bound.
       
       These results yield concrete guidance for corpus curation: binary filtering (remove all depth ≥ 1) is the principled choice for web text and code (K* ≤ 2), while academic text (K*=3) may benefit from finer depth stratification.
       K* localizes where depth-aware filtering yields diminishing returns, preventing practitioners from investing in graduated schemes beyond the discrimination boundary.
       The framework is extensible: new domains, models, or feature sets can be evaluated by rerunning the OBD pipeline without architectural changes.
       
       For the model collapse community, K* ≤ 3 under generator-independent features implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations; monitoring beyond this point is unlikely to reveal additional degradation structure.
       Open directions include scaling beyond 32B (where the trend toward K*=1 may accelerate), characterizing mixed prompt/retrain chains that better approximate real-world contamination, and exploring richer feature representations that may push K* higher on structured domains.
       ```

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen synthetic text is recursively fed back as input, the resulting multi-generational content degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively prompting a fixed model to generate from its own prior outputs); we study prompt-chains, which isolate text-intrinsic information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nThis gap cuts across multiple communities: binary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}), membership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and data selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.\n\nKnowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.\nFor data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principled choice.\nModel collapse theory predicts monotonic quality degradation across generations, but existing analyses are population-level and provide no per-text measure of where degradation saturates.\n$\\Kempirical$ fills this gap by localizing the generation at which adjacent outputs become empirically indistinguishable.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees monotonic cumulative information loss across generations; together with Markov chain convergence (adjacent transitions $Y_{k-1} \\to Y_k$ become increasingly similar as the chain approaches stationarity), this motivates a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$, whose actual value must be determined empirically.\nWe deliberately employ simple, interpretable features to establish a conservative lower bound on the discrimination boundary: any more expressive classifier can only raise $\\Kempirical$.\nThe DeBERTa results (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) confirm this design: neural representations push $\\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains, validating the lower-bound interpretation and demonstrating that our measurements are not artifacts of weak classifiers.\nWe present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{First empirical measurement of $\\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\\theta{=}0.60$: 4/6 families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.008$ $\\auc$ for generator identity).\n  Zero-shot frontier LLMs likewise collapse beyond $\\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\\% (vs.\\ 20\\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Domain ordering}: arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across tested configurations; code (Python) matches web text depth despite a different modality, confirmed by multi-source pooling (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-independent features within continuation mode enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments are fragile under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$); as a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts binary ${>}$ graduated filtering for web text (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
        "numLines": 38,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 38
      }
    },

        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.\nSeveral configurations are near-boundary (CI margins 0.2--4.5\\,pp above $\\theta$), and cross-model transfer at 7B preserves aggregate accuracy (96--100\\% pairwise AUC retention) but not boundary-level assignments (4/6 off-diagonal pairs fall below $\\theta$), underscoring that $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is a robust population-level finding but fragile at the per-configuration level.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (3v4 $\\auc = 0.682$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), confirming across three architecture families that retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ is protocol-determined rather than architecture-specific.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is thus the operationally relevant floor for data curation; retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ the upper bound under controlled conditions.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay, domain dependence, and the two-tier ceiling.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$, $\\Kempirical{=}7$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\nThe domain ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}): domains with richer formatting conventions (LaTeX markup, citation patterns, mathematical notation) retain more depth-distinguishing signal per generation.\nThe classifier gap confirms a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure: DeBERTa $=$ OBD on C4 and Code but $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}).\nThis gap reflects classifier capacity rather than an intrinsic domain ceiling; more expressive representations may further raise $\\Kempirical$.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale trends ($\\leq$32B).}\nLightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.621 (14B) to 0.618 (32B), a total drop of 0.8\\,pp (${\\sim}$0.4\\,pp per 10$\\times$ scale increase).\nThe trend suggests eventual decay toward $\\Kempirical{=}1$ at larger scales, but three data points are insufficient for reliable extrapolation; we report this as a qualitative observation rather than a quantitative prediction.\n\n\\paragraph{Why $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ under generator-independent features.}\nThe Data Processing Inequality guarantees that $\\Ktheory$ is finite but provides no upper bound.\nDPI motivates cumulative decay across the generational chain; the empirical finding $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ across all domains indicates that 15-dimensional statistical features exhaust depth-distinguishing information within two to three generations.\nThis is consistent with the JSD cliff on C4 ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$; Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): token-level divergence decays exponentially, leaving insufficient signal for the OBD classifier beyond the first few boundaries.\nRicher feature sets or learned representations may push $\\Kempirical$ higher, as the DeBERTa two-tier results suggest.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, indicating that $\\Kempirical$ is an intrinsic property of the domain rather than of any particular generator.\nFor corpus curation, filtering strategies can be calibrated by domain type alone: binary for web text and code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), finer stratification for academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$).\nFor the model collapse community, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal web-scale contamination likely involves a mixture of prompt-chain and retrain-chain processes.\nOur results bound the two extremes: prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ provides a text-intrinsic floor, while retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$--$7$ reflects training dynamics.\nThe mixed-chain $\\Kempirical$ is expected to fall between these bounds, but whether prompt-chain laundering can mask retrain-chain depth signals remains an open question.\n\n",

        "startLine": 1,

        "content": "% EMNLP 2026 Main - ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage{pifont}\n\\newcommand{\\cmark}{\\ding{51}}\n\\newcommand{\\xmark}{\\ding{55}}\n\\usepackage{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\\newcommand{\\tpm}{\\ensuremath{\\,\\pm\\,}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using generator-independent statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across models ($\\leq$32B); within this hierarchy, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text emerges as the dominant mode (7/9 continuation-mode configurations, four of six families).\nA fine-tuned DeBERTa classifier cannot break this ceiling on low-complexity domains (C4, Code) but extends $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (feature-set-dependent two-tier structure).\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\\% accuracy) but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ is fragile.\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 10-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (6 architecture families; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B, Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$); a $\\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5 of 10 total configurations (4 of 9 continuation-mode); though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the five single-configuration families.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",
        "numLines": 97,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 97
      }
    },

        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nUnder prompt-chain recursion ($\\leq$32B), the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across 7/9 continuation-mode configurations (4/6 families); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated ceilings across all four domains.\nDeBERTa matches OBD on low-complexity domains but extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv, establishing a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure.\nRetrain-chain experiments confirm $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ across three architecture families, placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the text-intrinsic floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the protocol-dependent upper bound.\n\nThese results yield concrete guidance for corpus curation: binary filtering (remove all $\\text{depth} \\geq 1$) is the principled choice for web text and code ($\\Kempirical \\leq 2$), while academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) may benefit from finer depth stratification.\n$\\Kempirical$ localizes where depth-aware filtering yields diminishing returns, preventing practitioners from investing in graduated schemes beyond the discrimination boundary.\nThe framework is extensible: new domains, models, or feature sets can be evaluated by rerunning the OBD pipeline without architectural changes.\n\nFor the model collapse community, $\\Kempirical \\leq 3$ under generator-independent features implies that population-level quality metrics (TTR decline, mode narrowing) carry the most diagnostic value in the first two to three generations; monitoring beyond this point is unlikely to reveal additional degradation structure.\nOpen directions include scaling beyond 32B (where the trend toward $\\Kempirical{=}1$ may accelerate), characterizing mixed prompt/retrain chains that better approximate real-world contamination, and exploring richer feature representations that may push $\\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains.\n",

        "totalLines": 14
      }

25	1v2 & .6955 & [.686,\,.704]\rlap{$^*$} & .6539 & [.645,\,.663]\rlap{$^*$} & .6145 & [.605,\,.625]\rlap{$^*$} & .6112 & [.602,\,.621]\rlap{$^*$} & .6168 & [.608,\,.626]\rlap{$^*$} \\
26	2v3 & .5893 & [.580,\,.599] & .5514 & [.542,\,.563] & .5496 & [.540,\,.560] & .5609 & [.551,\,.571] & .5492 & [.539,\,.559] \\
27	3v4 & .5435 & & .5152 & & .5091 & & .5409 & & .5276 & \\
28	4v5 & .5340 & & .5136 & & .5045 & & .5141 & & .5085 & \\
29	\bottomrule
30	\multicolumn{11}{l}{\footnotesize $^*$Permutation $p < 0.001$ for all 1v2 comparisons shown.} \\
31	\multicolumn{11}{l}{\footnotesize $^\dagger$Rewrite mode (ablation); excluded from continuation-mode $\Kempirical$ claim.} \\
32	\multicolumn{11}{l}{\footnotesize $^\ddagger$Generator: Qwen2.5-7B (fp16); scorer: Qwen2.5-1.5B.}
33	\end{tabular}
34	\end{table*}
35	
36	\begin{figure}[t]
37	\centering
38	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_auc_decay.pdf}
39	\caption{Pairwise $\auc$ decay across depth pairs for model-mode configurations (solid) and decoding strategies (dashed). The horizontal dashed line marks $\theta = 0.60$.}
40	\label{fig:auc_decay}
41	\end{figure}
42	
43	Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay} show $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for 7/9 continuation-mode C4 configurations across four of six tested architecture families (4 Qwen + 3 non-Qwen; 1v2 $\auc$ range: 0.6112--0.6539; all CI lower bounds $> \theta$; permutation $p < 0.001$).
44	The 1v2 vs.\ 2v3 AUC gap ranges from 5.0 to 10.6\,pp across configurations, exhibiting a sharp cliff at the boundary: all 2v3 values fall below $\theta$ while 1v2 values remain 1--10\,pp above it.
45	Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B are competence-limited at $\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\auc$: 0.598, CI $[0.588, 0.608]$; and 0.594, respectively): both achieve near-perfect 0v1 separation but fail to sustain 1v2 above $\theta$.
46	Among the remaining non-Qwen families, Pythia and OLMo yield clear $\Kempirical{=}2$; LLaMA-3.1-8B is borderline (1v2 $\auc = 0.6123$, CI lower $= 0.603$).
47	An independent F1@0.50 analysis confirms the same boundary for all configurations (Appendix~\ref{sec:f1_validation}).
48	All four decoding strategies likewise yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}); the 1v2 spread due to generation mode ($\Delta = 8.4$\,pp) exceeds the spread across models ($\Delta = 3.9$\,pp).
49	$\Kempirical{=}2$ persists from 1.5B through 14B despite reduced 0v1 signal at larger scales; a base-vs.-instruct ablation at 1.5B also yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:boundary_extended}).
50	
51	\begin{figure}[t]
52	\centering
53	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_kstar_theta.pdf}
54	\caption{$\Kempirical(\theta)$ sensitivity curve for six configurations. $\Kempirical$: maximum discriminable depth; $\theta$: AUC decision threshold. All configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (dashed line). At $\theta{=}0.70$, all drop to $\Kempirical{=}1$.}
55	\label{fig:kstar_theta}
56	\end{figure}
57	
58	Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta} shows $\Kempirical$ as a function of $\theta$: web text $\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across a 5\,pp window ($\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$), with a gradual transition to $\Kempirical{=}3$ at $\theta{=}0.55$ (two Qwen configurations) and $\Kempirical{=}1$ at $\theta{=}0.70$.
59	The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ web $\geq$ cross-scorer) is preserved across the full $\theta$ range (Table~\ref{tab:theta_sensitivity}; Figure~\ref{fig:theta_sensitivity} in Appendix); this ordering invariance provides a more robust characterization than absolute $\Kempirical$ values, which shift with $\theta$.
60	A $\theta$-free changepoint analysis (PELT) agrees within $\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (94.6\%), with domain ordering preserved (Appendix~\ref{sec:changepoint_validation}).
61	
62	%% ====================================================================
63	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer and Its Limits}
64	\label{sec:transfer}
65	
66	\begin{figure}[t]
67	\centering
68	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
69	\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap. Each cell shows 6-class ordinal accuracy when trained on the row model and tested on the column model, divided by in-domain accuracy (retention rate). Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo: 90--92\%; Qwen-rewrite$\to$continuation: 61--63\%.}
70	\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}
71	\end{figure}
72	
73	Aggregate transfer is strong: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\% ordinal accuracy across generators at $\leq$1.5B (Figure~\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap}; Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer}), and 96--100\% mean pairwise AUC at 7B (Appendix~\ref{sec:7b_transfer}).
74	However, boundary-level transfer is fragile: at 7B, 4/6 off-diagonal 1v2 pairs fall below $\theta$, so $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments do not transfer at the boundary even as overall discriminability improves with scale.
75	Qwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\%), consistent with reliance on mode-specific artifacts.
76	
77	\paragraph{Cross-scorer validation.}
78	Replacing the self-scorer with Pythia-1.4B or OLMo-1B preserves feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$; Table~\ref{tab:crossscorer}) and $\Kempirical$ in 20/22 cross-scorer configurations on C4 and arXiv; the two exceptions are the most marginal boundary configurations (AUC within 0.002 of $\theta$).
79	For arXiv, Pythia-1.4B cross-scoring preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ for 3/4 generators (2v3 $\auc$: 0.608--0.632); Qwen-7B drops to borderline $\Kempirical{=}2$ ($\Delta = -2.7$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}).
80	A three-way ANOVA confirms that Depth-Pair dominates variance ($\eta^2_p = 0.998$); Scorer is statistically significant but practically minor ($\eta^2_p = 0.192$; Table~\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}); F1@0.50 independently confirms scorer independence (Appendix~\ref{sec:f1_validation}).
81	Replacing the 1.5B reference scorer with Qwen2.5-7B (4.7$\times$ parameters) preserves $\Kempirical$ identically across all tested configurations on two domains ($\Delta\,\auc < 1$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:scorer_scale}).
82	
83	%% ====================================================================
84	\subsection{Feature Ordering and Generation Mode}
85	\label{sec:feature_degradation}
86	\label{sec:generation_mode}
87	
88	Among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings are highly correlated ($\rho \geq 0.85$; Table~\ref{tab:top_features}).
89	The top-3 features (\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std}, \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}) are shared across all three families: autoregressive generation progressively damps surprisal variability; however, no single feature subset of size ${<}8$ reliably sustains $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_subset}).
90	A feature group ablation (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_ablation}) confirms that surprisal dominates on arXiv but the boundary signal on C4 is carried by feature combinations rather than any single indicator.
91	Qwen-rewrite diverges (\texttt{freq\_entropy} dominates; 1v2 $\auc = 0.696$ vs.\ continuation's $0.611$, $\Delta = +8.4$\,pp), consistent with mode-specific artifacts and its poor cross-model transfer (61--63\%); continuation mode is therefore recommended as the canonical condition.
92	
93	%% ====================================================================
94	\subsection{Neural Probe}
95	\label{sec:neural_probe}
96	
97	Replacing the 15D features with frozen encoder hidden states (MLP probe, 5{,}000 texts per depth; Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}), Qwen achieves $K^*_{\text{neural}} = 3$ (2v3 $\auc = 0.616$), while Pythia and OLMo remain at $K^*_{\text{neural}} = 2$ ($\auc_{2v3}$: 0.596, 0.589).
98	The Qwen-only elevation reflects self-familiarity artifacts that transfer poorly (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}); the 15D statistical features yield consistent $\Kempirical = 2$ across all families.
99	
100	\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}
101	DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifiers on C4 at 1.5B, 14B, and 32B raise 1v2 $\auc$ over LightGBM (1.5B: $0.705$ vs.\ $0.626$; 14B: $0.678$ vs.\ $0.621$; 32B: $0.694$ vs.\ $0.618$), yet $\Kempirical{=}2$ is preserved (all 2v3 CI lower ${<}\,\theta$; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}); DeBERTa also yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ across three generation configurations at 1.5B (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_1b5_configs}).
102	LightGBM $\auc$ shifts by ${<}\,0.5$\,pp across 1.5B--32B, confirming scale and representation invariance on web text.
103	The classifier gap establishes a two-tier ceiling: zero on C4 and Code (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}2$, matching OBD; Code 2v3 $\auc = 0.593$, CI upper $= 0.602$), but $+1$ on Wikipedia (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}3$; OLMo excluded) and $+1$ on arXiv (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}4$, 3v4 $\auc = 0.624$, CI lower ${>}\,\theta$; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}); cross-transfer on Wikipedia mirrors the OBD pattern ($\Kempirical{=}3$ drops to ${\sim}2$; Table~\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_transfer}).
104	
105	\noindent\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}
106	\centering
107	\footnotesize
108	\captionof{table}{DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\ LightGBM on C4 generations at three scales. Both classifiers yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at 1.5B, 14B, and 32B.}
109	\label{tab:deberta_comparison}
110	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
111	\toprule
112	& \textbf{0v1} & \textbf{1v2} & \textbf{2v3} & \textbf{3v4} & \textbf{4v5} \\
113	\midrule
114	LightGBM (1.5B) & ---  & .626 & .549 & ---  & --- \\
115	DeBERTa (1.5B)  & .992 & .705 & .599 & ---  & --- \\
116	\midrule
117	LightGBM (14B)  & .960 & .621 & .549 & .524 & --- \\
118	DeBERTa (14B)   & .994 & .678 & .590 & .565 & --- \\
119	\midrule
120	LightGBM (32B)  & .958 & .618 & .545 & .522 & .516 \\
121	DeBERTa (32B)   & .993 & .694 & .594 & .567 & .543 \\
122	\bottomrule
123	\end{tabular}
124	\end{minipage}
125	
126	%% ====================================================================
127	\subsection{Cross-Domain Variation}
128	\label{sec:cross_domain}
129	
130	The preceding experiments use C4 (general web text).
131	To test whether $\Kempirical$ reflects domain structure or model identity, we evaluate on arXiv abstracts ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model) and Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$ per model) across six model configurations spanning four architecture families (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}).
132	
133	\paragraph{arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$): five-model cross-validation.}
134	All five domain-competent models yield $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, with boundary-pair $\auc$ (2v3) ranging from 0.6215 to 0.6545, all exceeding $\theta$.
135	The elevated signal reflects higher per-generation distributional shift (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\ ${\sim}0.01$ for C4): formulaic features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, mathematical notation) degrade slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth signal across four architecture families and 1.4B--8B parameters.
136	Removing LaTeX markup reduces 2v3 AUC by ${\sim}$1.8\,pp but preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ (CI lower $= 0.610 > \theta$; Table~\ref{tab:latex_ablation}); content-level properties dominate.
137	OLMo-1B is a capacity failure (0v1 JSD $= 0.33$, near-total information loss), not a counter-example.
138	Mistral-7B provides the strongest domain-dependence evidence: $\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4 (Section~\ref{sec:boundary_cases}) but $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv: domain structure alone can shift $\Kempirical$ by two levels within the same model.
139	
140	\paragraph{Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$): single-generation saturation.}
141	With scorer confound controlled (Pythia-1.4B for Qwen generators; Qwen-1.5B, itself cross-family, for all others), all six models yield $\Kempirical{=}1$ on Wikipedia: 1v2 $\auc$ ranges from 0.498 (OLMo) to 0.595 (LLaMA), all below $\theta$.
142	Wikipedia represents the simplest domain tested; its encyclopedic regularity provides minimal structural signal beyond the 0v1 boundary, and a single generation pass suffices for complete saturation.
143	Same-family scoring inflates Qwen $\Kempirical$ to 2 on Wikipedia ($\Delta = +2.4$\,pp); cross-family scoring controls this bias without affecting C4 results (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}).
144	
145	\paragraph{Code ($\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$): cross-modality confirmation.}
146	Python functions from CodeSearchNet~\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ for four models (Qwen-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-8B, Mistral-7B; 1v2 $\auc$: 0.615--0.739; Table~\ref{tab:code_crossmodel_kstar}), but $\Kempirical{=}1$ for capacity-limited Pythia-1.4B ($\auc = 0.598$).
147	Code matches C4 with the same sharp 2v3 collapse (JSD ratio $11.7$--$13.5\times$); cross-family scorer validation confirms the boundary is text-intrinsic ($\Delta \leq 1.9$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:code_domain}).
148	
149	\paragraph{Multi-source generator mixing.}
150	When outputs from three generators per domain are pooled (pools vary by domain; Appendix~\ref{sec:multi_source_details}), domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ is preserved across all four domains: C4 $\Kempirical{=}2$ (unified 1v2 $\auc = 0.711$; generator identity adds $+0.008$), arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ (unified 2v3 $\auc = 0.625$, CI $[0.615, 0.640]$), Code $\Kempirical{=}2$, and Wikipedia $\Kempirical{=}1$.
151	Wikipedia $\Kempirical{=}1$ under multi-source independently confirms the cross-scorer correction (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}): the self-scorer confound inflates single-source Wikipedia to $\Kempirical{=}2$, but both cross-scorer and multi-source converge on $\Kempirical{=}1$.
152	
153	\noindent\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}
154	\centering
155	\footnotesize
156	\captionof{table}{Domain $\times$ Model $\Kempirical$ matrix (scorer-controlled). Wikipedia values use cross-family scorer (Pythia-1.4B). $^*$OLMo arXiv: capacity failure. $^\dagger$Same-family scorer yields $\Kempirical{=}2$; controlled value shown. $^\ddagger$Borderline: Pythia CI upper $= 0.603$, Mistral CI lower $= 0.603$.}
157	\label{tab:domain_kstar}
158	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
159	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
160	\toprule
161	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{C4} & \textbf{Code} & \textbf{arXiv} & \textbf{Wikipedia} \\
162	\midrule
163	Qwen-1.5B    & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1$^\dagger$ \\
164	Qwen-7B      & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1$^\dagger$ \\
165	Pythia-1.4B  & 2 & 1$^\ddagger$ & 3 & 1 \\
166	OLMo-1B      & 2 & --- & 1$^*$ & 1 \\
167	Gemma-2-2B   & 1 & --- & --- & --- \\
168	LLaMA-3.1-8B & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1 \\
169	Mistral-7B   & 1 & 2$^\ddagger$ & 3 & 1 \\
170	\bottomrule
171	\end{tabular}}
172	\end{minipage}
173	
174	\paragraph{Summary.}
175	\label{sec:binary_confidence}
176	$\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for web text holds across four of six families (1B--32B), four decoding strategies, and multi-source pools (Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B: $\Kempirical{=}1$; binary detectors: $K^*_{\text{binary}} \leq 1$, Table~\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}).
177	The domain ordering arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia is $\theta$-invariant; scale and architecture do not alter this ordering, confirming structural complexity as the primary determinant.
178	
```

Appendix~\\ref{sec:scorer_scale}).\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Feature Ordering and Generation Mode}\n\\label{sec:feature_degradation}\n\\label{sec:generation_mode}\n\nAmong continuation-mode models, single-feature $\\auc$ rankings are highly correlated ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$; Table~\\ref{tab:top_features}).\nThe top-3 features (\\texttt{surp\\_d1\\_std}, \\texttt{surp\\_std}, \\texttt{surp\\_d2\\_std}) are shared across all three families: autoregressive generation progressively damps surprisal variability; however, no single feature subset of size ${<}8$ reliably sustains $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:feature_subset}).\nA feature group ablation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:feature_ablation}) confirms that surprisal dominates on arXiv but the boundary signal on C4 is carried by feature combinations rather than any single indicator.\nQwen-rewrite diverges (\\texttt{freq\\_entropy} dominates; 1v2 $\\auc = 0.696$ vs.\\ continuation's $0.611$, $\\Delta = +8.4$\\,pp), consistent with mode-specific artifacts and its poor cross-model transfer (61--63\\%); continuation mode is therefore recommended as the canonical condition.\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Neural Probe}\n\\label{sec:neural_probe}\n\nReplacing the 15D features with frozen encoder hidden states (MLP probe, 5{,}000 texts per depth; Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}), Qwen achieves $K^*_{\\text{neural}} = 3$ (2v3 $\\auc = 0.616$), while Pythia and OLMo remain at $K^*_{\\text{neural}} = 2$ ($\\auc_{2v3}$: 0.596, 0.589).\nThe Qwen-only elevation reflects self-familiarity artifacts that transfer poorly (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}); the 15D statistical features yield consistent $\\Kempirical = 2$ across all families.\n\n\\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifiers on C4 at 1.5B, 14B, and 32B raise 1v2 $\\auc$ over LightGBM (1.5B: $0.705$ vs.\\ $0.626$; 14B: $0.678$ vs.\\ $0.621$; 32B: $0.694$ vs.\\ $0.618$), yet $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is preserved (all 2v3 CI lower ${<}\\,\\theta$; Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}); DeBERTa also yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ across three generation configurations at 1.5B (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_1b5_configs}).\nLightGBM $\\auc$ shifts by ${<}\\,0.5$\\,pp across 1.5B--32B, confirming scale and representation invariance on web text.\nThe classifier gap establishes a two-tier ceiling: zero on C4 and Code (DeBERTa $\\Kempirical{=}2$, matching OBD; Code 2v3 $\\auc = 0.593$, CI upper $= 0.602$), but $+1$ on Wikipedia (DeBERTa $\\Kempirical{=}3$; OLMo excluded) and $+1$ on arXiv (DeBERTa $\\Kempirical{=}4$, 3v4 $\\auc = 0.624$, CI lower ${>}\\,\\theta$; Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}); cross-transfer on Wikipedia mirrors the OBD pattern ($\\Kempirical{=}3$ drops to ${\\sim}2$; Table~\\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_transfer}).\n\n\\noindent\\begin{minipage}{\\columnwidth}\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\captionof{table}{DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\\ LightGBM on C4 generations at three scales. Both classifiers yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at 1.5B, 14B, and 32B.}\n\\label{tab:deberta_comparison}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\textbf{0v1} & \\textbf{1v2} & \\textbf{2v3} & \\textbf{3v4} & \\textbf{4v5} \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM (1.5B) & ---  & .626 & .549 & ---  & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa (1.5B)  & .992 & .705 & .599 & ---  & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM (14B)  & .960 & .621 & .549 & .524 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa (14B)   & .994 & .678 & .590 & .565 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM (32B)  & .958 & .618 & .545 & .522 & .516 \\\\\nDeBERTa (32B)   & .993 & .694 & .594 & .567 & .543 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{minipage}\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Cross-Domain Variation}\n\\label{sec:cross_domain}\n\nThe preceding experiments use C4 (general web text).\nTo test whether $\\Kempirical$ reflects domain structure or model identity, we evaluate on arXiv abstracts ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model) and Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$ per model) across six model configurations spanning four architecture families (Table~\\ref{tab:domain_kstar}).\n\n\\paragraph{arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$): five-model cross-validation.}\nAll five domain-competent models yield $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, with boundary-pair $\\auc$ (2v3) ranging from 0.6215 to 0.6545, all exceeding $\\theta$.\nThe elevated signal reflects higher per-generation distributional shift (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4): formulaic features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, mathematical notation) degrade slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth signal across four architecture families and 1.4B--8B parameters.\nRemoving LaTeX markup reduces 2v3 AUC by ${\\sim}$1.8\\,pp but preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (CI lower $= 0.610 > \\theta$; Table~\\ref{tab:latex_ablation}); content-level properties dominate.\nOLMo-1B is a capacity failure (0v1 JSD $= 0.33$, near-total information loss), not a counter-example.\nMistral-7B provides the strongest domain-dependence evidence: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4 (Section~\\ref{sec:boundary_cases}) but $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv: domain structure alone can shift $\\Kempirical$ by two levels within the same model.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$): single-generation saturation.}\nWith scorer confound controlled (Pythia-1.4B for Qwen generators; Qwen-1.5B, itself cross-family, for all others), all six models yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$ on Wikipedia: 1v2 $\\auc$ ranges from 0.498 (OLMo) to 0.595 (LLaMA), all below $\\theta$.\nWikipedia represents the simplest domain tested; its encyclopedic regularity provides minimal structural signal beyond the 0v1 boundary, and a single generation pass suffices for complete saturation.\nSame-family scoring inflates Qwen $\\Kempirical$ to 2 on Wikipedia ($\\Delta = +2.4$\\,pp); cross-family scoring controls this bias without affecting C4 results (Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}).\n\n\\paragraph{Code ($\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$): cross-modality confirmation.}\nPython functions from CodeSearchNet~\\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for four models (Qwen-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-8B, Mistral-7B; 1v2 $\\auc$: 0.615--0.739; Table~\\ref{tab:code_crossmodel_kstar}), but $\\Kempirical{=}1$ for capacity-limited Pythia-1.4B ($\\auc = 0.598$).\nCode matches C4 with the same sharp 2v3 collapse (JSD ratio $11.7$--$13.5\\times$); cross-family scorer validation confirms the boundary is text-intrinsic ($\\Delta \\leq 1.9$\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{sec:code_domain}).\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source generator mixing.}\nWhen outputs from three generators per domain are pooled (pools vary by domain; Appendix~\\ref{sec:multi_source_details}), domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ is preserved across all four domains: C4 $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (unified 1v2 $\\auc = 0.711$; generator identity adds $+0.008$), arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (unified 2v3 $\\auc = 0.625$, CI $[0.615, 0.640]$), Code $\\Kempirical{=}2$, and Wikipedia $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\nWikipedia $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under multi-source independently confirms the cross-scorer correction (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}): the self-scorer confound inflates single-source Wikipedia to $\\Kempirical{=}2$, but both cross-scorer and multi-source converge on $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\n\n\\noindent\\begin{minipage}{\\columnwidth}\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\captionof{table}{Domain $\\times$ Model $\\Kempirical$ matrix (scorer-controlled). Wikipedia values use cross-family scorer (Pythia-1.4B). $^*$OLMo arXiv: capacity failure. $^\\dagger$Same-family scorer yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$; controlled value shown. $^\\ddagger$Borderline: Pythia CI upper $= 0.603$, Mistral CI lower $= 0.603$.}\n\\label{tab:domain_kstar}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{C4} & \\textbf{Code} & \\textbf{arXiv} & \\textbf{Wikipedia} \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-1.5B    & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1$^\\dagger$ \\\\\nQwen-7B      & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1$^\\dagger$ \\\\\nPythia-1.4B  & 2 & 1$^\\ddagger$ & 3 & 1 \\\\\nOLMo-1B      & 2 & --- & 1$^*$ & 1 \\\\\nGemma-2-2B   & 1 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1 \\\\\nMistral-7B   & 1 & 2$^\\ddagger$ & 3 & 1 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{minipage}\n\n\\paragraph{Summary.}\n\\label{sec:binary_confidence}\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ for web text holds across four of six families (1B--32B), four decoding strategies, and multi-source pools (Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$; binary detectors: $K^*_{\\text{binary}} \\leq 1$, Table~\\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}).\nThe domain ordering arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia is $\\theta$-invariant; scale and architecture do not alter this ordering, confirming structural complexity as the primary determinant.\n",
